Wizards.com D&D 3.5 archive

Posted in RPG on 2017-06-29 — last updated 2023-04-05

During the run of Dungeons & Dragons third edition (2000-2007), Wizards of the Coast’s D&D website posted thousands of free articles including new spells, monster stats, maps, advice articles, news and errata. This article attempts to provide a searchable list of that content.

Elite Opponents

Original index: Elite Opponents

Revision Spotlight

Original index: Revision Spotlight

An explanation of the changes between D&D 3.0 and 3.5. Monte Cook once said that D&D 3.5 made too many small and confusing changes, and the length of this FAQ supports this.

Although the individual articles for this category are still online, the original index link has compiled all articles into one long article for convenience.

Notes

During the D&D 4th edition era, Wizards of the Coast stopped the practice of distributing massive amounts of free D&D material on their site, instead channelling that effort into the D&D Insider paid service. Gratefully, they allowed an archive of the 3rd edition D&D content to stay online.

After the launch of D&D 5th edition, the 3e archive was kept online but moved from www.wizards.com to archive.wizards.com. This had the result of breaking links around the web, since they didn’t set up redirects for the thousands of old URLs. HTTPS URLs would also attempt to redirect to the main site and fail.

As of 2017-06-29, the subdirectory /dnd/ was blocked by robots.txt on both www.wizards.com and archive.wizards.com. This had the result of blocking many articles from the Wayback Machine, generally older articles from before April 2003. Fortunately, when Wizards failed to maintain the old site in 2022, they also failed to maintain the robots.txt files, meaning that those pages are retained in the Wayback Machine.

A few pieces of the old site are no longer online. These are mainly the Original Adventures and the free AD&D book downloads. Many of these were released for sale when WotC discovered they could sell out-of-print titles in ebook format. Later they would pull all ebooks from retailers over piracy fears, frustrating some users who had already paid for books but not downloaded them. Fourth edition was a weird time for D&D.

Around 2022, Wizards of the Coast took archive.wizards.com offline. I’ve replaced the URLs here with archived versions from The Wayback Machine.
